# Point-in-time Restore Specification - revision 4.2

## 1. Status and authority

This revision is approved; the document control register is the only authority on which revision of any document is in force, never revision numbers, file dates or file names. A restore feed is governed by this specification together with the documents it incorporates: the feed readme (stream README), the catalog format guide (stream FMTGUIDE), the path conventions (stream PATHCONV), the media handling procedure (stream MEDIAPROC), the quarantine procedure (stream QPROC), the source authority matrix (stream AUTH) and the restore request (stream RRQ).

Sections 7.1 to 7.3 (pattern dialect) and section 9.2 (timestamp conversion) of revision 4.1 are carried forward unchanged and are not restated here. Every other part of revision 4.1 is superseded.

Where two sources state the same fact, the source authority matrix decides which one is of record. A source the matrix does not rank for a fact is never used for that fact, even when it is the newest or the most complete file in the feed.

## 2. The restore

The restore request names the feed, the share, the restore point and the request date. The restore point is a wall-clock time in the site time zone of the share (share registry, site_tz); when that wall time occurs twice, the first occurrence is meant. The end of the request date is 23:59:59 wall-clock time in the site time zone on the request date. The restore point date is the local site date of the restore point.

## 5. Backup set chain

5.1 Sets of the share. A set belongs to the share when its job name, trimmed of surrounding white space and compared case-insensitively, is a job that the job definitions assign to the share. A retired job definition carries no share. A set run under an old job name belongs to the share when the job rename crosswalk maps that old name to a job of the share and the set was queued before the crosswalk row's effective date (local site date).

5.2 Snapshot time. The snapshot time of a set is the instant of the snapshot line for that set in the log of the backup server that ran it (a line of the form 'snapshot {guid} created set=<set>'). Job queued and ended times are never snapshot times. A synthetic full (set type SYNTH) has no snapshot of its own: its snapshot time is the snapshot time of the set named in its consolidates column. The backup server register gives each server's time zone and, per agent build, the time notation its log uses from the build's installed instant onwards.

5.3 Status. The status of a set is its job history status, replaced by the latest catalog-check status change recorded for the set in the server log.

5.4 Crash-inconsistent snapshots. A set is crash-inconsistent when its snapshot instant lies inside a maintenance window of the share's array (share registry) whose STATUS is CONFIRMED and whose X-IMPACT is SNAPSHOT-CONSISTENCY. A window includes its start and excludes its end. When the storage array event log records actual-start and actual-end for the event's UID, that actual window replaces the calendar window.

5.5 Chain selection. Sets are ordered by snapshot time, ties by set identifier. A set is eligible when its snapshot time is at or before the restore point, its status is COMPLETED or PARTIAL and it is not crash-inconsistent. The base is the latest eligible FULL or SYNTH set. The differential is the latest eligible DIFF set whose snapshot is after the base, if any. The anchor is the differential, or the base when there is none. Every eligible INCR set after the anchor is applied in order, until a crash-inconsistent set after the anchor breaks the chain: no set after a break is applied. FAILED and CANCELLED sets never break the chain, because the next incremental is taken relative to the last set that completed. The effective point is the snapshot time of the last applied set.

5.6 Skipped sets. Every set of the share that is not applied is skipped with exactly one reason, the first that holds in this order: AFTER_RP (snapshot after the restore point), FAILED, CANCELLED, CRASH_INCONSISTENT, BEFORE_BASE (snapshot at or before the base snapshot), SUPERSEDED_BY_DIFF (snapshot at or before the anchor snapshot), CHAIN_BROKEN.

## 6. Catalog replay

6.1 Order. Entries of the applied sets are applied set by set in chain order and, within a set, in ascending seq. Entries are gathered from every catalog part of the feed; a set's entries may be spread over several parts, and parts list entries in no particular order.

6.2 Entry types. Identity of objects and the spelling rule are those of PATHCONV section 6. D creates a directory when it is absent and changes nothing otherwise. F replaces the version of an existing file and keeps its spelling; on an absent path it creates the file. H creates a file at path that shares the current version of the file at target. L creates a symbolic link at path whose raw target is target. W removes the object at path; with kind dir it removes the directory and everything under it. R moves the object at path to new_path; with kind dir it moves the directory and everything under it. X voids another entry of the same set (section 6.3).

6.3 Voids. An X entry names in its voids column the entry identifier it voids. A voided entry is not applied. The X entry and the entry it voids may be recorded in different parts.

6.4 Catalog errata. A correction in a catalog errata document replaces one field (sha256, size, mtime or container) of one entry before replay. Only corrections with status APPROVED in an errata revision in force apply; when several apply to the same entry and field, the one from the highest errata revision wins. Corrected values are written as lowercase hexadecimal, a byte count, an ISO 8601 UTC time and a canonical container identifier; a correction whose value is not written in that form is malformed and never applies, whatever its status or revision.

6.5 Uncommitted entries. An entry whose committed flag is false was not committed by its set and is not applied.

6.6 Rejected entries. An entry whose raw path is not under one of the client's roots for the share, or whose path climbs above the share root, is rejected and not applied (PATHCONV section 3).

## 7. Exclusion patterns

7.4 Catalogue revision. The exclusion catalogue in force is the revision with the highest number whose effective_from is on or before the restore point date. Its patterns apply when their scope is ALL or the share, their effective_from is on or before the restore point date and their retired_from is empty or after it. A pattern listed in the withdrawal register of any catalogue revision stops applying from the withdrawal's effective date, whichever revision is in force.

## 8. Scope and exclusions

Every file and symbolic link that exists at the effective point is either restored or excluded, never both. The first reason that holds, in this order, excludes it: OUT_OF_SCOPE (the path is not at or under one of the include prefixes of the share's restore profile in Appendix B; prefixes match whole components, case-insensitively), DISPOSED (the path is at or under the prefix of a records disposition with action DESTROY whose effective date is on or before the restore point date; detail is the lowest such hold identifier), EXCLUDED_PATTERN (a pattern in force matches the display path; detail is the matching pattern with the lowest identifier number), SYMLINK_ESCAPE (a link whose target leaves the share, PATHCONV section 5), QUARANTINED (QPROC section 4; detail is the sha256 of the version at the effective point), UNRECOVERABLE (no usable copy, MEDIAPROC section 2; detail is the container identifier after repack). Directories are neither restored nor excluded.

## 9. Version metadata

9.1 Mode. The restored mode is the permission bits of the restored entry with the set-user-ID and set-group-ID bits cleared and the sticky bit kept, written as four octal digits. Entries from Windows clients carry attributes instead of a mode: 0444 when the attributes contain R, 0644 otherwise.

9.3 Owner. A principal of the form uid:<n> is resolved in the local account register under the section of the host of the restored entry's client. Any other principal is resolved through the latest approved identity change effective at or before the restore point, otherwise through the identity map row valid at the restore point (valid_from included, valid_to excluded). A principal that nothing resolves, or an owner sentinel, gives UNMAPPED.

## 10. Allocation

Allocation is computed for the restore target volume named in the restore request (restore target register). A file of size at most resident_max allocates 0 bytes. Otherwise the size is rounded up to a whole number of clusters, using large_cluster when the size is at least large_threshold and small_cluster otherwise. Within a hard-link group only the first member in output order allocates; the others allocate 0. Symbolic links allocate 0.

## 11. Deliverables

11.1 CSV files are UTF-8 without a byte order mark, with LF line endings, a header row and RFC 4180 quoting (a field is quoted only when it contains a comma, a double quote, CR or LF; embedded quotes are doubled). The last line of each CSV file is a trailer '#custody=<digest> rows=<n>' where digest is the feed custody digest (README section 6) and n the number of data rows. Rows of the manifest and of the exclusions are ordered by the Unicode code points of the case-folded NFC display path, ties by the display path itself.

11.2 restore_manifest.csv has the columns path, kind, size, allocated, sha256, mode, mtime_utc, owner, source_set, container, media, copy, hardlink_group, link_target. path is the display path at the effective point; kind is file or symlink; size is in bytes; sha256 is lowercase hexadecimal; mtime_utc is YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ; source_set is the set of the entry whose version is restored; container is the container after repack; media and copy are the media and copy number chosen; hardlink_group is HL- followed by four digits, numbered from 0001 in output order of each group's first member, for files whose restored versions share client and file_id with at least one other restored file; link_target is the resolved target of a symbolic link. Fields that do not apply to a symbolic link (sha256, container, media, hardlink_group) are empty, its size, allocated and copy are 0. Fields that do not apply to a file (link_target) are empty.

11.3 exclusions.csv has the columns path, kind, reason, detail; detail is empty for OUT_OF_SCOPE and SYMLINK_ESCAPE. 11.4 media_pull_list.csv has the columns media_id, vault, copy, containers, read_bytes, read_gib: one row per media holding a chosen copy, containers counted once each, read_bytes the sum of their sizes, read_gib that sum divided by 1073741824 rounded half to even to three decimals, written with exactly three decimals. Rows are ordered by the vault priority of MEDIAPROC Appendix E on the request date, then media_id.

11.5 Both JSON files are UTF-8 objects whose keys appear exactly in the order listed here, at every level. restore_chain.json has the keys share, restore_point_utc, effective_point_utc, chain (applied sets in order, each an object with the keys set, type, snapshot_utc and role, role being base, diff or incremental), skipped (each skipped set as an object with the keys set, type, snapshot_utc and reason, ordered by snapshot then set) and custody_digest. 11.6 reconciliation.json has the keys sets (one object per applied set in chain order with the keys set, expected, entries, applied, voided, uncommitted and rejected), objects_at_effective_point, restored, excluded (an object whose keys are the six reasons in the order of section 8, zero included), rolled_back (files restored from an earlier entry under QPROC section 4), bytes_restored (sum of restored sizes), bytes_allocated (sum of allocations), hardlink_groups, media, containers and read_bytes (the pull list's row count, container total and byte total) and custody_digest. expected is the set's catalog entry control total of record (source authority matrix, set.entry_total); entries counts every catalog entry of the set in the feed; applied counts the entries actually applied; voided counts entries voided by an X entry; uncommitted and rejected count those entries. An X entry is counted in entries only: it is never counted as applied, voided, uncommitted or rejected. Counts and byte sums are JSON integers.

## 12. Values that look wrong but are valid

Files of tens of gibibytes, modification times after the snapshot (client clocks running ahead) and modification times decades old are valid and are restored as recorded. Quarantine follows QPROC and media selection follows MEDIAPROC.
